Function Families and Transformations · Framework v6.35

Section 4.1 Section 4.2 Section 4.3 Section 4.4 Section 4.5 Secure Summative By I-Can / Parallel View

Section 4.1: Recognizing Function Families

Learning target: Students will identify common function families from graphs, equations, tables, and contexts.

WTC

WTC 1

U4-S1-WTC-001 · WTC · Intro · 4.1-WTC-SHARED · Table + Graph · constructed/open response

Two mystery relationships A and B are shown in the table, and four familiar family graphs are shown below. Without using formal family tests, describe what stays predictable as x increases, choose a likely family for A and B, and name one graph feature you would look for to check each prediction.

xAB
021
152
284
3118
Answer / solution

A has constant first differences of 3, so linear is likely. B doubles, so exponential is likely. A line-like graph and an exponential growth curve would support those predictions.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct evidence for a function family using patterns in graphs, equations, tables, and contexts.

Expected evidence/job: A has constant first differences of 3, so linear is likely. B doubles, so exponential is likely. A line-like graph and an exponential growth curve would support those predictions.

Representation: Table + Graph

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes Example

Notes Example 1

U4-S1-EX-01 · Notes Example · Intro · 4.1-NOTES-EQUATION · Equation · constructed/open response

Classify the function family represented by $y=3|x-1|+1$. Give one feature of the equation that supports your classification.

Answer / solution

Absolute Value; the equation structure is characteristic of an absolute value function.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct evidence for a function family using patterns in graphs, equations, tables, and contexts.

Expected evidence/job: Absolute Value; the equation structure is characteristic of an absolute value function.

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes Example 3

U4-S1-EX-03 · Notes Example · Intro · 4.1-NOTES-GRAPH · Graph · constructed/open response

The four graphs below show familiar function families. Identify the family in the top-left, top-right, bottom-left, and bottom-right positions, in that order.

Answer / solution

Linear, quadratic, exponential, absolute value.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply family features to sort representations into linear, quadratic, exponential, absolute value, and other familiar forms.

Expected evidence/job: Linear, quadratic, exponential, absolute value.

Representation: Graph

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes Example 4

U4-S1-EX-04 · Notes Example · Intro · 4.1-NOTES-EVIDENCE · Multiple-representation reasoning · constructed/open response

An equation has the form $y=3|x-2|+1$ while a low-resolution graph looks like two nearly straight pieces. Identify the family and name the decisive evidence.

Answer / solution

It is absolute value. The absolute-value equation structure is decisive, and the two linear-looking arms are consistent with that family.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply family features to sort representations into linear, quadratic, exponential, absolute value, and other familiar forms.

Expected evidence/job: It is absolute value. The absolute-value equation structure is decisive, and the two linear-looking arms are consistent with that family.

Representation: Multiple-representation reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes Example 5

U4-S1-EX-05 · Notes Example · Intro · 4.1-NOTES-EQUATION · Equation · constructed/open response

Classify the function family represented by $y=|x-2|-1$. Give one feature of the equation that supports your classification.

Answer / solution

Absolute Value; the equation structure is characteristic of an absolute value function.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can determine which representation gives the strongest evidence for a function family when features seem similar.

Expected evidence/job: Absolute Value; the equation structure is characteristic of an absolute value function.

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes Example 6

U4-S1-EX-06 · Notes Example · Intro · 4.1-NOTES-TABLE · Table · constructed/open response

A table has constant second differences, while a cropped graph shows only one increasing side of the relationship. Which evidence is more decisive for the function family, and what family does it support?

Answer / solution

Constant second differences are stronger exact evidence and support a quadratic family; one cropped side of a graph can hide the turning point and symmetry.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can determine which representation gives the strongest evidence for a function family when features seem similar.

Expected evidence/job: Constant second differences are stronger exact evidence and support a quadratic family; one cropped side of a graph can hide the turning point and symmetry.

Representation: Table

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes YTI 2

U4-S1-YTI-02 · Notes YTI · Intro · 4.1-NOTES-GRAPH · Graph · constructed/open response

In the graph gallery, which position shows the exponential family? State one visible feature that distinguishes it from the linear graph.

Answer / solution

The bottom-left graph is exponential. Its rate of change is not constant; the curve grows increasingly steep instead of following one straight line.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct evidence for a function family using patterns in graphs, equations, tables, and contexts.

Expected evidence/job: Linear, quadratic, exponential, absolute value.

Representation: Graph

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes YTI 3

U4-S1-YTI-03 · Notes YTI · Intro · 4.1-NOTES-EVIDENCE · Multiple-representation reasoning · constructed/open response

A table has a constant ratio of 4, but the first few plotted points look almost collinear in a small graph window. Classify the family using the stronger evidence.

Answer / solution

A constant ratio of 4 supports an exponential relationship; the exact multiplicative table pattern is stronger evidence than the cropped graph window.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply family features to sort representations into linear, quadratic, exponential, absolute value, and other familiar forms.

Expected evidence/job: A constant ratio of 4 supports an exponential relationship; the exact multiplicative table pattern is stronger evidence than the cropped graph window.

Representation: Multiple-representation reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes YTI 4

U4-S1-YTI-04 · Notes YTI · Intro · 4.1-NOTES-EQUATION · Equation · constructed/open response

Classify the function family represented by $y=2\left(3\right)^x$. Give one feature of the equation that supports your classification.

Answer / solution

Exponential; the variable is in the exponent and the constant base is 3.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply family features to sort representations into linear, quadratic, exponential, absolute value, and other familiar forms.

Expected evidence/job: Exponential; the variable is in the exponent and the constant base is 3.

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes YTI 6

U4-S1-YTI-06 · Notes YTI · Intro · 4.1-NOTES-GRAPH · Graph · constructed/open response

In the graph gallery, the top-right and bottom-right graphs are both symmetric and have a lowest point. What visible feature best distinguishes the two families?

Answer / solution

The top-right graph has a smooth curved vertex, so it is quadratic. The bottom-right graph has a sharp corner, so it is absolute value.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can determine which representation gives the strongest evidence for a function family when features seem similar.

Expected evidence/job: The top-right graph has a smooth curved vertex, so it is quadratic. The bottom-right graph has a sharp corner, so it is absolute value.

Representation: Graph

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 1 10

U4-S1-P1-10 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · 4.1-P-EVIDENCE · Multiple-representation reasoning · constructed/open response

An equation is $y=2(x+1)^2-2$, while a rough sketch looks almost V-shaped near its minimum. Use the family features in the representations. Which evidence is decisive for the family?

Answer / solution

Quadratic; the squared input in vertex form and the smooth parabolic turning point are decisive, not the rough local appearance.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply family features to sort representations into linear, quadratic, exponential, absolute value, and other familiar forms.

xRelationship B
03
22
41
Answer / solution

Exit Ticket B 2

U4-S1-ExitTicketB-02 · Exit Ticket B · Review · 4.1-SYS-SOLUTION-TYPE · Equation reasoning · constructed/open response

Without solving for an ordered pair, determine whether the system $y=2x+3$ and $2y=4x+10$ has one solution, no solution, or infinitely many solutions. Explain using slope and intercept information.

Answer / solution

No solution. The second equation is $y=2x+5$, so the lines have the same slope but different y-intercepts and are parallel.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Solve systems graphically and by elimination while interpreting the meaning of different solution types.

Expected evidence/job: Correctly identifies a no-solution system from equal slopes and different intercepts and explains why the lines do not intersect.

Representation: Equation reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket B 3

U4-S1-ExitTicketB-03 · Exit Ticket B · Review · 4.1-SYS-SOLUTION-TYPE · Equation reasoning · constructed/open response

Determine whether the system $3x-6y=9$ and $x-2y=3$ has one solution, no solution, or infinitely many solutions. Explain how the equations show the relationship between the two lines.

Answer / solution

Infinitely many solutions. Multiplying $x-2y=3$ by 3 gives $3x-6y=9$, so both equations represent the same line.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Solve systems graphically and by elimination while interpreting the meaning of different solution types.

Expected evidence/job: Recognizes equivalent equations as the same line and interprets that relationship as infinitely many solutions.

Representation: Equation reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket C 2

U4-S1-ETC-02 · Exit Ticket C · Mastery/Portfolio · 4.1-MG03-CONTEXT-COMPARE · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

Plan A starts at \$12 and increases by \$3 per hour. Plan B starts at \$20 and increases by \$2 per hour. Compare their starting values and rates, determine when the plans cost the same, and state which plan is cheaper after 10 hours.

Answer / solution

Plan A has slope 3 and intercept 12; Plan B has slope 2 and intercept 20. $12+3h=20+2h$ gives $h=8$. After 10 hours, A costs \$42 and B costs \$40, so B is cheaper.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare linear relationships shown in different representations using slope, intercepts, and contextual meaning.

Expected evidence/job: Compares slope and intercept in context, solves a crossover condition, and uses the comparison to make a contextual conclusion.

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket C 3

U4-S1-ETC-03 · Exit Ticket C · Mastery/Portfolio · 4.1-MG03-POINTS-EQ-COMPARE · Points + Equation · constructed/open response

Relationship A passes through $(0,-2)$ and $(4,6)$. Relationship B is $y=1.5x+1$. Compare their slopes and starting values. Which changes faster, and what happens to their outputs at $x=6$?

Answer / solution

A has slope 2 and intercept -2. B has slope 1.5 and intercept 1. A changes faster; B starts higher. At $x=6$, both have output 10.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare linear relationships shown in different representations using slope, intercepts, and contextual meaning.

Expected evidence/job: Extracts comparable slope/intercept features from two different representations and uses them to compare behavior at a specified input.

Representation: Points + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket C 4

U4-S1-ETC-04 · Exit Ticket C · Mastery/Portfolio · 4.1-MG03-CLAIM-CRITIQUE · Equation + Table · constructed/open response

A student says Relationship B changes faster than $y=2x+5$ because the outputs in B are larger. Use the table to critique the claim with slope and starting-value evidence.

xRelationship B
010
212
414
Answer / solution

The claim is false. The equation has slope 2 and intercept 5. Relationship B has slope 1 and intercept 10. B starts higher, but the equation changes faster.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare linear relationships shown in different representations using slope, intercepts, and contextual meaning.

Expected evidence/job: Distinguishes starting value from rate of change and critiques a misleading comparison using evidence from both representations.

Representation: Equation + Table

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Section 4.2: Translations

Learning target: Students will graph and write translated functions using key points and key features.

WTC

WTC 1

U4-S2-WTC-001 · WTC · Intro · 4.2-WTC-SHARED · Table + Graph · constructed/open response

A parabola has been moved without changing its shape. Use the point pairs to describe how every point moved. Predict where the vertex moved, then compare your prediction with the two graphs below.

parent pointnew point
(-1,1)(2,-1)
(0,0)(3,-2)
(1,1)(4,-1)
Answer / solution

Every point moved right 3 and down 2; the vertex moves from $(0,0)$ to $(3,-2)$. The graph pair matches that translation.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can build translated graphs by moving key points and key features from a parent function.

Expected evidence/job: Every point moved right 3 and down 2; the vertex moves from $(0,0)$ to $(3,-2)$. The graph pair matches that translation.

Representation: Table + Graph

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes YTI 4

U4-S2-YTI-04 · Notes YTI · Intro · 4.2-NOTES-MISCONCEPTION · Equation reasoning · constructed/open response

A student says $y=(x+2)^2+2$ moves $y=x^2$ right 2 and up 2. Identify and correct the horizontal-shift error.

Answer / solution

Inside the input, the sign acts oppositely: $x+2$ shifts the graph left 2. The vertical shift up 2 is correct.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can use horizontal and vertical shifts to write equations for translated functions.

Practice Set 2 11

U4-S2-P2-11 · Practice Set 2 · Intro · 4.2-P-GRAPH · Graph · constructed/open response

Use the absolute-value graph pair to locate the parent vertex and translated vertex. Then write the translated equation and describe both shifts.

Answer / solution

The parent vertex is $(0,0)$ and the translated vertex is $(-1,-3)$. The equation is $y=|x+1|-3$, so the graph shifts left 1 and down 3.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can use horizontal and vertical shifts to write equations for translated functions.

Expected evidence/job: The right graph is $y=|x+2|+3$; it is shifted left 2 units and up 3 units.

Representation: Graph

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 2 12

U4-S2-P2-12 · Practice Set 2 · Intro · 4.2-P-MISCONCEPTION · Equation reasoning · constructed/open response

A student says $y=|x+1|-4$ translates the parent function right 1 and down 4. Identify the error and give the correct translation.

Answer / solution

The horizontal direction is reversed. The correct translation is left 1 and down 4; the sign inside the input acts opposite the visible horizontal movement.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can use horizontal and vertical shifts to write equations for translated functions.

Expected evidence/job: The horizontal direction is reversed. The correct translation is left 1 and down 4; the sign inside the input acts opposite the visible horizontal movement.

Representation: Equation reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 3

Practice Set 3 1

U4-S2-P3-01 · Practice Set 3 · Intro · 4.2-P-MISCONCEPTION · Equation reasoning · constructed/open response

A student says $y=(x-3)^2+2$ moves $y=x^2$ left 3 units and up 2 units. Identify and correct the horizontal-shift error.

Answer / solution

Inside the input, the sign acts oppositely: $x-3$ shifts the graph right 3 units. The vertical shift up 2 units is correct.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can build translated graphs by moving key points and key features from a parent function.

Expected evidence/job: Inside the input, the sign acts oppositely: $x-3$ shifts the graph right 3 units. The vertical shift up 2 units is correct.

Representation: Equation reasoning

The left graph is a parent function and the right graph is a translation. Describe the horizontal and vertical shift, then write a matching translated equation.

Answer / solution

Right 2 and down 3, $y=(x-2)^2-3$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can build translated graphs by moving key points and key features from a parent function.

Expected evidence/job: Right 2 and down 3, $y=(x-2)^2-3$.

Representation: Graph

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Extra Practice 5

U4-S2-PX-05 · Extra Practice · Intro · 4.2-P-MISCONCEPTION · Equation reasoning · constructed/open response

A student says $y=(x+2)^2+4$ moves $y=x^2$ right 2 units and up 4 units. Identify and correct the horizontal-shift error.

Answer / solution

Inside the input, the sign acts oppositely: $x+2$ shifts the graph left 2 units. The vertical shift up 4 units is correct.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can build translated graphs by moving key points and key features from a parent function.

Expected evidence/job: Inside the input, the sign acts oppositely: $x+2$ shifts the graph left 2 units. The vertical shift up 4 units is correct.

Representation: Equation reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Extra Practice 10

U4-S2-PX-10 · Extra Practice · Intro · 4.2-P-MISCONCEPTION · Equation reasoning · constructed/open response

A student says $y=|x-2|+2$ translates the parent function left 2 and up 2. Identify the error and give the correct translation.

Answer / solution

The horizontal direction is reversed. The correct translation is right 2 and up 2; the sign inside the input acts opposite the visible horizontal movement.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can use horizontal and vertical shifts to write equations for translated functions.

Expected evidence/job: The horizontal direction is reversed. The correct translation is right 2 and up 2; the sign inside the input acts opposite the visible horizontal movement.

Representation: Equation reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Extra Practice 14

U4-S2-PX-14 · Extra Practice · Intro · 4.2-P-GRAPH · Graph · constructed/open response

Use the graph pair to predict the signs that must appear in the translated equation. Explain the horizontal-sign convention.

Answer / solution

The graph is shifted left 2 units and up 3 units, so a matching equation is $y=(x+2)^2+3$. The horizontal sign appears opposite the direction of the shift because it changes the input.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can predict how changing values in an equation moves a graph left, right, up, or down.

Expected evidence/job: The graph is shifted left 2 units and up 3 units, so a matching equation is $y=(x+2)^2+3$. The horizontal sign appears opposite the direction of the shift because it changes the input.

Representation: Graph

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket B 4

U4-S2-ExitTicketB-04 · Exit Ticket B · Review · 4.2-LIN-INEQ-POINT-TEST · Equation + solution-region reasoning · constructed/open response

For the inequality $y\le 2x+2$, determine whether $(2,5)$ is in the shaded solution region. Show the substitution and explain your conclusion.

Answer / solution

Yes. Substituting gives $5\le 2(2)+2=6$, which is true, so $(2,5)$ lies in the solution region.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Graph and interpret linear inequalities.

Expected evidence/job: Tests a point accurately and connects a true inequality statement to membership in the graphed solution region.

Representation: Equation + solution-region reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket C 2

U4-S2-ETC-02 · Exit Ticket C · Mastery/Portfolio · 4.2-MG02-CONTEXT-EQ · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A gym charges an \$18 sign-up fee and \$6 per class. Write a linear equation for total cost $C$ after $n$ classes, and identify the slope and y-intercept in context.

Answer / solution

$C=6n+18$. The slope 6 means \$6 per class, and the y-intercept 18 is the sign-up cost before any classes.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Graph linear functions and write linear equations from slope, intercepts, tables, graphs, and situations.

Expected evidence/job: Translates contextual rate and initial value into a linear equation and interprets both parameters.

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket C 3

U4-S2-ETC-03 · Exit Ticket C · Mastery/Portfolio · 4.2-MG02-TWO-POINT-EQ · Points + Equation · constructed/open response

A line passes through $(-2,1)$ and $(2,9)$. Determine the slope and write the equation in slope-intercept form.

Answer / solution

The slope is $(9-1)/(2+2)=2$. Using either point gives $b=5$, so the equation is $y=2x+5$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Graph linear functions and write linear equations from slope, intercepts, tables, graphs, and situations.

Expected evidence/job: Computes slope from two points and determines the corresponding slope-intercept equation accurately.

Representation: Points + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket C 4

U4-S2-ETC-04 · Exit Ticket C · Mastery/Portfolio · 4.2-MG02-EQ-GRAPH · Equation + Graph production · constructed/open response

For $y=-3x+4$, identify the y-intercept and use the slope to determine one additional point. Describe how those points determine the graph.

Answer / solution

The y-intercept is $(0,4)$. A slope of -3 can be shown by moving right 1 and down 3 to $(1,1)$. Plot the two points and draw the line through them.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Graph linear functions and write linear equations from slope, intercepts, tables, graphs, and situations.

Expected evidence/job: Uses slope-intercept features to produce valid graph points and explains how they define the line.

Representation: Equation + Graph production

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Section 4.3: Transformations

Learning target: Students will analyze and graph transformations involving stretches, compressions, and reflections.

WTC

WTC 1

U4-S3-WTC-001 · WTC · Intro · 4.3-WTC-SHARED · Table + Graph · constructed/open response

A parent absolute-value relationship is changed. Use the table to describe what happened to the outputs, predict the new graph shape, and then compare your prediction with the graph pair.

xparent ynew y
-22-4
-11-2
000
11-2
22-4
Answer / solution

Each output is multiplied by $-2$, so the graph reflects across the x-axis and vertically stretches by 2. The graph pair matches the table: both transformed vertices remain at $(0,0)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design transformed graphs using stretches, compressions, reflections, and translations of parent functions.

Expected evidence/job: Each output is multiplied by $-2$, so the graph reflects across the x-axis and vertically stretches by 2. The graph pair matches the table: both transformed vertices remain at $(0,0)$.

Representation: Table + Graph

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes Example 5

U4-S3-EX-05 · Notes Example · Intro · 4.3-NOTES-MISCONCEPTION · Conceptual diagnosis · constructed/open response

A student calls $g(x)=\frac{1}{3}f(x)$ a vertical stretch because the coefficient is positive. Correct the claim.

Answer / solution

Because the output multiplier has magnitude between 0 and 1, the graph is vertically compressed by factor $1/3$; positivity only tells us there is no x-axis reflection.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can assess whether an equation, graph, or table represents the same transformed function.

Expected evidence/job: Because the output multiplier has magnitude between 0 and 1, the graph is vertically compressed by factor $1/3$; positivity only tells us there is no x-axis reflection.

Representation: Conceptual diagnosis

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes YTI 3

U4-S3-YTI-03 · Notes YTI · Intro · 4.3-NOTES-EQUIV · Equation + Table · constructed/open response

For $f(x)=|x|$, compare $g(x)=-4f(x)$ with the table of points $(-2,-8), (-1,-4), (0,0), (1,-4), (2,-8)$. Do the equation and table represent the same transformed function? Explain.

Answer / solution

Yes. Multiplying each parent output $|x|$ by -4 gives the listed table values, so both represent a reflection across the x-axis with vertical stretch factor 4.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply transformation rules to key points and features across function families.

Expected evidence/job: Yes. Multiplying each parent output $|x|$ by -4 gives the listed table values, so both represent a reflection across the x-axis with vertical stretch factor 4.

Representation: Equation + Table

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes YTI 4

U4-S3-YTI-04 · Notes YTI · Intro · 4.3-NOTES-MISCONCEPTION · Conceptual diagnosis · constructed/open response

A student says multiplying $f(x)$ by 12 moves its graph right 12 units. Correct the transformation description.

Answer / solution

Multiplying the outputs by 12 creates a vertical stretch by factor 12; a right shift would require changing the input, such as $f(x-12)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply transformation rules to key points and features across function families.

Expected evidence/job: Multiplying the outputs by 12 creates a vertical stretch by factor 12; a right shift would require changing the input, such as $f(x-12)$.

Representation: Conceptual diagnosis

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 1 8

U4-S3-P1-08 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · 4.3-P-MISCONCEPTION · Conceptual diagnosis · constructed/open response

Explain why multiplying only the vertex of a parabola by a scale factor while leaving the other points unchanged does not produce a valid vertical stretch or compression.

Answer / solution

A vertical scale changes every output relative to the x-axis; all y-coordinates must be multiplied by the factor, not just the vertex.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply transformation rules to key points and features across function families.

Expected evidence/job: A vertical scale changes every output relative to the x-axis; all y-coordinates must be multiplied by the factor, not just the vertex.

Representation: Conceptual diagnosis

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 1 19

U4-S3-PracticeSet1-19 · Practice Set 1 · Review · 4.3-SYS-INEQ · Graph reasoning · constructed/open response

Describe the solution region for the system $y\gt2x+1$ and $y\le -x+4$, including which boundary is dashed and which is solid.

Answer / solution

Shade points above the dashed line $y=2x+1$ and on/below the solid line $y=-x+4$; the overlap is the solution region.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Analyze and interpret systems of linear inequalities.

Expected evidence/job: Shade points above the dashed line $y=2x+1$ and on/below the solid line $y=-x+4$; the overlap is the solution region.

Practice Set 1 24

U4-S3-PracticeSet1-24 · Practice Set 1 · Mastery · 4.3-SLOPE · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

Find and interpret the slope from $(1,-3)$ to $(3,-5)$. Treat $x$ as hours and $y$ as miles from a reference point.

Answer / solution

$m=-2/2=-1$. The position changes by -1 miles per hour.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Interpret slope as a measure of constant rate of change.

Expected evidence/job: $m=-2/2=-1$. The position changes by -1 miles per hour.

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 2

Practice Set 2 1

U4-S3-P2-01 · Practice Set 2 · Intro · 4.3-P-EQUIV · Equation + Table · constructed/open response

For $f(x)=|x|$, compare $g(x)=-3f(x)$ with the table of points $(-2,-6), (-1,-3), (0,0), (1,-3), (2,-6)$. Do the equation and table represent the same transformed function? Explain.

Answer / solution

Yes. Multiplying each parent output $|x|$ by -3 gives the listed table values, so both represent a reflection across the x-axis with vertical stretch factor 3.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design transformed graphs using stretches, compressions, reflections, and translations of parent functions.

Expected evidence/job: Yes. Multiplying each parent output $|x|$ by -3 gives the listed table values, so both represent a reflection across the x-axis with vertical stretch factor 3.

Representation: Equation + Table

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 2 2

U4-S3-P2-02 · Practice Set 2 · Intro · 4.3-P-MISCONCEPTION · Conceptual diagnosis · constructed/open response

A student says multiplying $f(x)$ by 4 moves its graph right 4 units. Correct the transformation description.

Answer / solution

Multiplying the outputs by 4 creates a vertical stretch by factor 4; a right shift would require changing the input, such as $f(x-4)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design transformed graphs using stretches, compressions, reflections, and translations of parent functions.

Expected evidence/job: Multiplying the outputs by 4 creates a vertical stretch by factor 4; a right shift would require changing the input, such as $f(x-4)$.

Representation: Conceptual diagnosis

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 2 12

U4-S3-P2-12 · Practice Set 2 · Intro · 4.3-P-MISCONCEPTION · Conceptual diagnosis · constructed/open response

Explain why multiplying only the vertex of a parabola by a scale factor of 3 while leaving the other points unchanged does not produce a valid vertical stretch.

Answer / solution

A vertical stretch by factor 3 must multiply every output value by 3. Changing only the vertex breaks the common transformation rule across the function.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply transformation rules to key points and features across function families.

Expected evidence/job: A vertical stretch by factor 3 must multiply every output value by 3. Changing only the vertex breaks the common transformation rule across the function.

Representation: Conceptual diagnosis

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 2 13

U4-S3-P2-13 · Practice Set 2 · Intro · 4.3-P-POINTS · Table · constructed/open response

xclaimed $g(x)$

-1-0.5
00
1-0.5
2-1
Answer / solution

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can assess whether an equation, graph, or table represents the same transformed function.

Expected evidence/job: The correct transformed values are -2, -0.5, 0, -0.5, -2; the entry at $x=2$ should be -2.

Representation: Table

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 2 17

U4-S3-P2-17 · Practice Set 2 · Intro · 4.3-P-MISCONCEPTION · Conceptual diagnosis · constructed/open response

A student reflects only the points with x-values greater than -2 across the x-axis and calls the result a reflection of the whole function. Diagnose the error.

Answer / solution

A reflection of the function across the x-axis changes every output $y$ to $-y$; points across the entire domain must be reflected, not just a restricted subset.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can assess whether an equation, graph, or table represents the same transformed function.

Expected evidence/job: A reflection of the function across the x-axis changes every output $y$ to $-y$; points across the entire domain must be reflected, not just a restricted subset.

Representation: Conceptual diagnosis

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 2 19

U4-S3-PracticeSet2-19 · Practice Set 2 · Review · 4.3-SYS-INEQ · Graph reasoning · constructed/open response

Describe the solution region for the system $y\gt x-2$ and $y\le x-2$, including which boundary is dashed and which is solid.

Answer / solution

Shade points above the dashed line $y=x-2$ and on/below the solid line $y=x-2$; the overlap is the solution region.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Analyze and interpret systems of linear inequalities.

Expected evidence/job: Shade points above the dashed line $y=x-2$ and on/below the solid line $y=x-2$; the overlap is the solution region.

Representation: Graph reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 2 20

Describe the solution region for the system $y\gt -x+2$ and $y\le 2x+4$, including which boundary is dashed and which is solid.

Answer / solution

Shade points above the dashed line $y=-x+2$ and on/below the solid line $y=2x+4$; the overlap is the solution region.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Analyze and interpret systems of linear inequalities.

Expected evidence/job: Shade points above the dashed line $y=-x+2$ and on/below the solid line $y=2x+4$; the overlap is the solution region.

Representation: Graph reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 3 11

U4-S3-P3-11 · Practice Set 3 · Intro · 4.3-P-MISCONCEPTION · Conceptual diagnosis · constructed/open response

A student calls $g(x)=\frac12 f(x)$ a vertical stretch because the coefficient is positive. Correct the claim.

Answer / solution

Because the output multiplier has magnitude between 0 and 1, the graph is vertically compressed by factor $1/2$; positivity only tells us there is no x-axis reflection.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply transformation rules to key points and features across function families.

Expected evidence/job: Because the output multiplier has magnitude between 0 and 1, the graph is vertically compressed by factor $1/2$; positivity only tells us there is no x-axis reflection.

Representation: Conceptual diagnosis

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 3 16

U4-S3-P3-16 · Practice Set 3 · Intro · 4.3-P-MISCONCEPTION · Conceptual diagnosis · constructed/open response

A student doubles only the y-values for points with positive x and calls the result a vertical stretch by 2. Explain the error.

Answer / solution

A vertical stretch multiplies every output by 2. Points with negative x, zero x, and positive x must all be transformed consistently.

Alignment / evidence

Practice Set 3 19

U4-S3-PracticeSet3-19 · Practice Set 3 · Review · 4.3-SYS-INEQ · Graph reasoning · constructed/open response

Describe the solution region for the system $y\gt x+2$ and $y\le-2x+5$, including which boundary is dashed and which is solid.

Answer / solution

Shade above the dashed line $y=x+2$; shade on or below the solid line $y=-2x+5$; the overlap is the solution region.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Analyze and interpret systems of linear inequalities.

Expected evidence/job: Shade above the dashed line $y=x+2$; shade on or below the solid line $y=-2x+5$; the overlap is the solution region.

Representation: Graph reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 3 22

U4-S3-PracticeSet3-22 · Practice Set 3 · Mastery · 4.3-SLOPE · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

Find and interpret the slope from $(1,1)$ to $(3,-1)$. Treat $x$ as hours and $y$ as miles from a reference point.

Answer / solution

$m=-2/2=-1$. The position changes by -1 miles per hour.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Interpret slope as a measure of constant rate of change.

Expected evidence/job: $m=-2/2=-1$. The position changes by -1 miles per hour.

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Extra Practice 4

U4-S3-PX-04 · Extra Practice · Intro · 4.3-P-EQUIV · Equation + Table · constructed/open response

For $f(x)=|x|$, compare $g(x)=3f(x)$ with the table of points $(-2,6),(-1,3),(0,0),(1,3),(2,5)$. Do the equation and table represent the same transformed function? Explain.

Answer / solution

No. The point $(2,5)$ does not follow $g(x)=3|x|$; its output should be 6, so the table and equation are not the same transformed function.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design transformed graphs using stretches, compressions, reflections, and translations of parent functions.

Expected evidence/job: No. The point $(2,5)$ does not follow $g(x)=3|x|$; its output should be 6, so the table and equation are not the same transformed function.

Representation: Equation + Table

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Extra Practice 5

U4-S3-PX-05 · Extra Practice · Intro · 4.3-P-MISCONCEPTION · Conceptual diagnosis · constructed/open response

A student says $g(x)=-f(x)$ reflects the graph across the y-axis. Correct the claim.

Answer / solution

Multiplying every output by $-1$ reflects the graph across the x-axis. A y-axis reflection would change the input to $f(-x)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design transformed graphs using stretches, compressions, reflections, and translations of parent functions.

Expected evidence/job: Multiplying every output by $-1$ reflects the graph across the x-axis. A y-axis reflection would change the input to $f(-x)$.

Representation: Conceptual diagnosis

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

For $f(x)=|x|$, compare $g(x)=-1.5f(x)$ with the table of points $(-2,-3), (-1,-1.5), (0,0), (1,-1.5), (2,-3)$. Do the equation and table represent the same transformed function? Explain.

Answer / solution

Yes. Multiplying each parent output $|x|$ by -1.5 gives the listed table values, so both represent a reflection across the x-axis with vertical stretch factor 1.5.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can assess whether an equation, graph, or table represents the same transformed function.

Exit Ticket A 3

U4-S3-ETA-03 · Exit Ticket A · Intro · 4.3-ETA-MISCONCEPTION · Conceptual diagnosis · constructed/open response

A student reflects only the points with x-values less than -1 across the x-axis and calls the result a reflection of the whole function. Diagnose the error.

Answer / solution

A reflection of the function across the x-axis changes every output $y$ to $-y$; points across the entire domain must be reflected, not just a restricted subset.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can assess whether an equation, graph, or table represents the same transformed function.

Exit Ticket B 2

U4-S3-ExitTicketB-02 · Exit Ticket B · Review · 4.3-SYS-INEQ-REGION · Graph reasoning · constructed/open response

For the system $y\ge x-1$ and $y\lt-2x+5$, state which boundary is solid and which is dashed, describe the overlap region, and give one point in the solution region.

Answer / solution

The boundary $y=x-1$ is solid and $y=-2x+5$ is dashed. Shade above/on the first line and below the second. For example, $(0,0)$ satisfies both inequalities.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Analyze and interpret systems of linear inequalities.

Expected evidence/job: Correctly identifies both boundary types, the intersection of the two half-planes, and a valid common solution point.

Representation: Graph reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket B 3

U4-S3-ExitTicketB-03 · Exit Ticket B · Review · 4.3-SYS-INEQ-FEASIBILITY · Equation reasoning · constructed/open response

Determine whether the system $y\ge x+3$ and $y\le x-1$ has any solutions. Explain without graphing every point.

Answer / solution

No. For the same $x$, a value of $y$ cannot be at least $x+3$ and at most $x-1$ because $x+3\gt x-1$ for every $x$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Analyze and interpret systems of linear inequalities.

Expected evidence/job: Recognizes an empty overlap region from incompatible parallel boundaries and explains why no point can satisfy both inequalities.

Representation: Equation reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket B 4

U4-S3-ExitTicketB-04 · Exit Ticket B · Review · 4.3-SYS-INEQ-POINT-TEST · Equation + region reasoning · constructed/open response

Does the point $(2,1)$ satisfy the system $y\gt-x+2$ and $y\le 0.5x+1$? Check both inequalities and explain what the result means on the graph.

Answer / solution

Yes. $1\gt-2+2=0$ and $1\le 0.5(2)+1=2$. Therefore $(2,1)$ lies in the overlap of the two solution regions.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Analyze and interpret systems of linear inequalities.

Expected evidence/job: Checks both inequalities correctly and interprets satisfying both as membership in the system solution region.

Representation: Equation + region reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket C 3

A temperature drops 18°F over 6 hours. A student reports the slope as $+3$°F per hour. Determine the correct slope and explain why the sign matters.

Answer / solution

The correct slope is $-18/6=-3$°F per hour. The negative sign is required because temperature is decreasing as time increases.

Alignment / evidence

Expected evidence/job: Uses context to assign the correct signed rate and explains why the sign communicates direction of change.

Exit Ticket C 4

U4-S3-ETC-04 · Exit Ticket C · Mastery/Portfolio · 4.3-MG01-POINT-RATE-PREDICT · Points + Context · constructed/open response

A tank-volume graph passes through $(2,75)$ and $(5,60)$, where $x$ is minutes and $y$ is gallons. Find and interpret the slope, then predict the change in volume over the next 4 minutes if the constant-rate model continues.

Answer / solution

The slope is $(60-75)/(5-2)=-5$ gallons per minute. The tank is losing 5 gallons each minute, so over 4 more minutes the volume would change by -20 gallons.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Interpret slope as constant rate of change across tables, graphs, and contexts and judge whether a rate is reasonable.

Expected evidence/job: Calculates and interprets a signed contextual slope from two points and uses the constant rate for a reasonable local prediction.

Representation: Points + Context

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Section 4.4: Polynomial Operations

Learning target: Students will add, subtract, and multiply polynomial expressions.

WTC

WTC 1

U4-S4-WTC-001 · WTC · Intro · 4.4-WTC-SHARED · Area model · constructed/open response

A rectangle has side lengths $x+3$ and $x+4$. Use the area model to explain how the four partial areas combine into one polynomial for total area. Then describe what must change if one side becomes $x-2$ instead.

Answer / solution

The partial areas are $x^2,4x,3x,12$, so total area is $x^2+7x+12$. Replacing $x+3$ by $x-2$ makes the second row negative: $-2x$ and $-8$, changing the product to $x^2+2x-8$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can formulate polynomial sums, differences, and products from area, perimeter, and expression contexts.

Expected evidence/job: The partial areas are $x^2,4x,3x,12$, so total area is $x^2+7x+12$. Replacing $x+3$ by $x-2$ makes the second row negative: $-2x$ and $-8$, changing the product to $x^2+2x-8$.

Representation: Area model

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes Example 6

U4-S4-EX-06 · Notes Example · Intro · 4.4-NOTES-TABLE · Table + Equation · constructed/open response

Show that $(x-1)(x+4)$ and $x^2+3x-4$ are equivalent by multiplying the binomials.

Answer / solution

$(x-1)(x+4)=x^2+4x-x-4=x^2+3x-4$, so the two forms are equivalent.

Alignment / evidence

Practice Set 2 4

A student simplifies $(5x^2 + 4x - 6)-(3x^2 - 4x + 3)$ as $2x^2 - 3$. Identify the subtraction error and give the correct result.

Answer / solution

The negative sign must distribute to every term in the second polynomial. The correct result is $2x^2 + 8x - 9$.

Practice Set 2 11

U4-S4-P2-11 · Practice Set 2 · Intro · 4.4-P-ERROR · Error analysis · constructed/open response

A student combines $3x^2+7x+2x^2$ as $12x^2$. Explain the error and simplify correctly.

Answer / solution

Only like terms combine: $3x^2+2x^2=5x^2$, while $7x$ stays separate. The result is $5x^2+7x$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can use structure and like terms to add, subtract, and multiply polynomial expressions.

A student simplifies $(4x^2+3x-2)-(x^2-5x+1)$ as $3x^2-2x-1$. Identify the error and correct the result.

Answer / solution

Practice Set 3 15

U4-S4-P3-15 · Practice Set 3 · Intro · 4.4-P-AREA · Area model · constructed/open response

Use the partial area model to explain why $(x+2)(x-6)$ and $x^2-4x-12$ are equivalent forms.

Answer / solution

The four cell areas are $x^2$, $-6x$, $2x$, and $-12$; their sum is $x^2-4x-12$, matching the product $(x+2)(x-6)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can extend polynomial operations to equivalent expressions written in different forms.

Expected evidence/job: The four cell areas are $x^2$, $-6x$, $2x$, and $-12$; their sum is $x^2-4x-12$, matching the product $(x+2)(x-6)$.

Representation: Area model

Warm-Up 1

Warm-Up 1 1

U4-S4-WU1-01 · Warm-Up 1 · Prior/Review · ALG-DIAMOND-INTEGER · Diamond · constructed/open response

Complete the Diamond Problem. The top is the product of the side numbers and the bottom is their sum.

Answer / solution

Side numbers 4 and -7 give product -28 and sum -3; fill the blank cell(s) accordingly.

Alignment / evidence

Expected evidence/job: Side numbers 4 and -7 give product -28 and sum -3; fill the blank cell(s) accordingly.

Exit Ticket B 2

U4-S4-ExitTicketB-02 · Exit Ticket B · Review · 4.4-SYS-SUB-DIRECT · Equation · constructed/open response

Solve the system $x=2y+1$ and $3x+y=17$ by substitution. Show the substitution step.

Answer / solution

Substitute $x=2y+1$: $3(2y+1)+y=17$, so $7y=14$ and $y=2$. Then $x=5$. The solution is $(5,2)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Solve systems using substitution and choose efficient forms for solving.

Expected evidence/job: Uses the isolated variable directly for substitution and solves the resulting one-variable equation accurately.

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket B 3

U4-S4-ExitTicketB-03 · Exit Ticket B · Review · 4.4-SYS-SUB-CHOOSE · Equation reasoning · constructed/open response

For $2x+y=11$ and $x-y=1$, identify the equation and variable you would isolate first for an efficient substitution. Then solve the system.

Answer / solution

A convenient choice is $x-y=1\Rightarrow y=x-1$. Then $2x+(x-1)=11$, so $x=4$ and $y=3$. The solution is $(4,3)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Solve systems using substitution and choose efficient forms for solving.

Expected evidence/job: Selects a low-friction isolation step, substitutes correctly, and solves the system while justifying the efficiency choice.

Representation: Equation reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket B 4

U4-S4-ExitTicketB-04 · Exit Ticket B · Review · 4.4-SYS-SUB-EFFICIENCY · Equation reasoning · constructed/open response

Solve $4x+2y=18$ and $y=x$ by substitution. Explain why substitution is an efficient choice for this system.

Answer / solution

Substitute $y=x$: $4x+2x=18$, so $x=3$ and $y=3$. Substitution is efficient because one variable is already isolated.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Solve systems using substitution and choose efficient forms for solving.

Expected evidence/job: Recognizes an already-isolated variable, uses it efficiently, and solves the system accurately.

Representation: Equation reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Exit Ticket C 2

U4-S4-ETC-02 · Exit Ticket C · Mastery/Portfolio · 4.4-MG02-GRAPH-FEATURE-EQ · Graph features + Equation · constructed/open response

A line has y-intercept 3 and passes through $(4,11)$. Determine its slope and write the equation in slope-intercept form.

Answer / solution

Using $(0,3)$ and $(4,11)$, the slope is $(11-3)/(4-0)=2$. The equation is $y=2x+3$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Graph linear functions and write linear equations from slope, intercepts, tables, graphs, and situations.

Exit Ticket C 3

U4-S4-ETC-03 · Exit Ticket C · Mastery/Portfolio · 4.4-MG02-CONTEXT-EQ · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A taxi ride costs \$5 to start and \$2.50 per mile. Write an equation for cost $C$ after $m$ miles and determine the cost after 6 miles.

Answer / solution

$C=2.5m+5$. At 6 miles, $C=2.5(6)+5=20$, so the cost is \$20.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Graph linear functions and write linear equations from slope, intercepts, tables, graphs, and situations.

Expected evidence/job: Translates rate and starting value into a linear model and uses the model for an in-context output.

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

The equation is $y=-1.5x+6$. The table values are $(0,6)$, $(2,3)$, and $(4,0)$.

Section 4.5: Polynomial Structure and Quadratic Graphs

Learning target: Students will connect polynomial expressions to quadratic graphs and key features.

WTC

WTC 1

U4-S5-WTC-001 · WTC · Intro · 4.5-WTC-SHARED · Table + Graph · constructed/open response

The table and graph describe the same quadratic relationship. Identify at least three features that can be seen in one or both representations, and explain which representation makes each feature easiest to notice.

xy
03
10
2-1
30
43
Answer / solution

Examples: vertex $(2,-1)$ is especially clear on the graph/table minimum; x-intercepts 1 and 3 are clear where y=0; axis of symmetry $x=2$ is clear from mirrored table values and graph symmetry; y-intercept is $(0,3)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct connections between polynomial structure, quadratic graphs, and key features.

Expected evidence/job: Examples: vertex $(2,-1)$ is especially clear on the graph/table minimum; x-intercepts 1 and 3 are clear where y=0; axis of symmetry $x=2$ is clear from mirrored table values and graph symmetry; y-intercept is $(0,3)$.

Notes Example 5

U4-S5-EX-05 · Notes Example · Intro · 4.5-NOTES-CLAIM · Conceptual · constructed/open response

A student says the factored form $y=(x-1)(x-5)$ gives a y-intercept of 1 because the first factor contains 1. Critique the claim.

Answer / solution

The factors reveal x-intercepts, not the y-intercept. At $x=0$, $y=(-1)(-5)=5$, so the y-intercept is $(0,5)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can critique whether a polynomial expression supports a claimed vertex, intercept, axis of symmetry, or direction of opening.

Expected evidence/job: The factors reveal x-intercepts, not the y-intercept. At $x=0$, $y=(-1)(-5)=5$, so the y-intercept is $(0,5)$.

Representation: Conceptual

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes YTI 4

U4-S5-YTI-04 · Notes YTI · Intro · 4.5-NOTES-CLAIM · Conceptual · constructed/open response

A student claims that the constant term of a quadratic always gives the vertex. Critique the claim using $y=x^2 - 4x + 2$.

Answer / solution

The constant term 2 gives the y-intercept $(0,2)$, not the vertex. This quadratic has vertex $(2,-2)$.

Alignment / evidence

Notes YTI 5

U4-S5-YTI-05 · Notes YTI · Intro · 4.5-NOTES-FACTORED · Equation · constructed/open response

A student claims the axis of symmetry of $y=(x+3)(x-1)$ is $x=1$ because 1 appears in a factor. Assess the claim.

Answer / solution

The claim is false. The x-intercepts are -3 and 1, so the axis of symmetry is their midpoint, $x=-1$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can critique whether a polynomial expression supports a claimed vertex, intercept, axis of symmetry, or direction of opening.

Expected evidence/job: The claim is false. The x-intercepts are -3 and 1, so the axis of symmetry is their midpoint, $x=-1$.

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Notes YTI 6

U4-S5-YTI-06 · Notes YTI · Intro · 4.5-NOTES-EXPANDED · Equation · constructed/open response

For $y=-2x^2+3x+4$, critique the claim that the graph opens upward because the constant term is positive.

Answer / solution

The claim is false. The leading coefficient is -2, so the parabola opens downward. The constant term 4 gives the y-intercept $(0,4)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can critique whether a polynomial expression supports a claimed vertex, intercept, axis of symmetry, or direction of opening.

Expected evidence/job: The claim is false. The leading coefficient is -2, so the parabola opens downward. The constant term 4 gives the y-intercept $(0,4)$.

Representation: Equation

Practice Set 1 8

U4-S5-P1-08 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · 4.5-P-CLAIM · Conceptual · constructed/open response

A student says $y=(x-2)(x+3)$ has x-intercepts 2 and 3. Critique the claim.

Answer / solution

Set each factor equal to zero. The x-intercepts are $x=2$ and $x=-3$; the sign inside each factor is opposite the root.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can predict graph features from equivalent quadratic expressions.

Expected evidence/job: Set each factor equal to zero. The x-intercepts are $x=2$ and $x=-3$; the sign inside each factor is opposite the root.

Representation: Conceptual

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 1 9

U4-S5-P1-09 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · 4.5-P-FACTORED · Equation · constructed/open response

For $y=(x+1)(x-3)$, predict the x-intercepts and axis of symmetry before graphing.

Answer / solution

The x-intercepts are $x=-1$ and $x=3$; the axis of symmetry is $x=1$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can predict graph features from equivalent quadratic expressions.

Expected evidence/job: The x-intercepts are $x=-1$ and $x=3$; the axis of symmetry is $x=1$.

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 1 10

U4-S5-P1-10 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · 4.5-P-EXPANDED · Equation · constructed/open response

For $y=x^2 + x + 2$, identify the y-intercept and the direction of opening.

Answer / solution

The y-intercept is $(0,2)$, and the parabola opens upward because the leading coefficient is 1.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can predict graph features from equivalent quadratic expressions.

Expected evidence/job: The y-intercept is $(0,2)$, and the parabola opens upward because the leading coefficient is 1.

Representation: Equation

Practice Set 1 13

U4-S5-P1-13 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · 4.5-P-TABLE · Table · constructed/open response

A student claims the table has axis of symmetry $x=3$. Use the paired table values to critique the claim and identify the correct vertex.

xy
05
12
21
32
45
Answer / solution

The claim is false. Matching y-values occur equally far from $x=2$, so the axis is $x=2$ and the vertex is $(2,1)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can critique whether a polynomial expression supports a claimed vertex, intercept, axis of symmetry, or direction of opening.

Expected evidence/job: The claim is false. Matching y-values occur equally far from $x=2$, so the axis is $x=2$ and the vertex is $(2,1)$.

Representation: Table

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 1 18

U4-S5-P1-18 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · 4.5-P-CLAIM · Conceptual · constructed/open response

A student claims $y=-x^2+3x+3$ opens upward because the constant term is positive. Critique the claim.

Answer / solution

The leading coefficient is $-1$, so the parabola opens downward. The constant term 3 gives the y-intercept, not the opening direction.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can critique whether a polynomial expression supports a claimed vertex, intercept, axis of symmetry, or direction of opening.

Expected evidence/job: The leading coefficient is $-1$, so the parabola opens downward. The constant term 3 gives the y-intercept, not the opening direction.

Representation: Conceptual

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 1 19

U4-S5-PracticeSet1-19 · Practice Set 1 · Review · 4.5-SYS-MODEL · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

Adult tickets cost \$8 and student tickets cost \$5. A school sold 10 tickets for \$65. Write and solve a system to determine how many of each type were sold.

Answer / solution

Let $a+s=10$ and $8a+5s=65$. Solving gives 5 adult tickets and 5 student tickets.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Create and solve systems that model real-world situations.

Expected evidence/job: Let $a+s=10$ and $8a+5s=65$. Solving gives 5 adult tickets and 5 student tickets.

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 1 22

U4-S5-PracticeSet1-22 · Practice Set 1 · Mastery · 4.5-LIN-MODEL · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A linear model for a school fundraiser is $M=1.5d+20$, where $d$ is days and $M$ is hundreds of dollars. Predict $M$ at $d=8$ and interpret the slope and intercept.

Answer / solution

$M=1.5(8)+20=32$, so the model predicts \$3200. The slope means \$150 per day and the intercept means \$2000 at day 0.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Use linear models to represent and interpret real-world data.

Expected evidence/job: $M=1.5(8)+20=32$, so the model predicts \$3200. The slope means \$150 per day and the intercept means \$2000 at day 0.

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 2 2

U4-S5-P2-02 · Practice Set 2 · Intro · 4.5-P-CLAIM · Conceptual · constructed/open response

A student claims that the constant term of a quadratic always gives the vertex. Critique the claim using $y=x^2 - 6x + 6$.

Answer / solution

The constant term 6 gives the y-intercept $(0,6)$, not the vertex. This quadratic has vertex $(3,-3)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct connections between polynomial structure, quadratic graphs, and key features.

Expected evidence/job: The constant term 6 gives the y-intercept $(0,6)$, not the vertex. This quadratic has vertex $(3,-3)$.

Representation: Conceptual

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 2 12

U4-S5-P2-12 · Practice Set 2 · Intro · 4.5-P-CLAIM · Conceptual · constructed/open response

A student says the axis of symmetry of $y=(x+2)(x-4)$ is $x=4$. Critique the claim.

Answer / solution

The axis lies halfway between the zeros $x=-2$ and $x=4$, so it is $x=1$, not $x=4$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can predict graph features from equivalent quadratic expressions.

Expected evidence/job: The axis lies halfway between the zeros $x=-2$ and $x=4$, so it is $x=1$, not $x=4$.

Representation: Conceptual

Practice Set 3

Practice Set 3 1

U4-S5-P3-01 · Practice Set 3 · Intro · 4.5-P-CLAIM · Conceptual · constructed/open response

A student claims the axis of symmetry of $y=x^2+6x+5$ is $x=6$ because the middle coefficient is 6. Critique the claim.

Answer / solution

The middle coefficient alone is not the axis. Using $x=-b/(2a)$ gives $x=-6/2=-3$, so the axis of symmetry is $x=-3$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct connections between polynomial structure, quadratic graphs, and key features.

Expected evidence/job: The middle coefficient alone is not the axis. Using $x=-b/(2a)$ gives $x=-6/2=-3$, so the axis of symmetry is $x=-3$.

Representation: Conceptual

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

Practice Set 3 22

U4-S5-PracticeSet3-22 · Practice Set 3 · Mastery · 4.5-LIN-MODEL · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A linear model for a school fundraiser is $M=1.5d+20$, where $d$ is days and $M$ is hundreds of dollars. Predict $M$ at $d=7$ and interpret the slope and intercept.

Answer / solution

$M=1.5(7)+20=30.5$, so the model predicts \$3050. The slope means \$150 per day and the intercept means \$2000 at day 0.

Alignment / evidence

Exit Ticket B 2

U4-S5-ExitTicketB-02 · Exit Ticket B · Review · 4.5-SYS-MODEL-SNACKS · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A school sells 12 snack packs. Basic packs cost \$4 and deluxe packs cost \$7. The total sales are \$66. Write and solve a system to determine how many of each pack were sold.

Answer / solution

Let $b$ be basic and $d$ deluxe. $b+d=12$ and $4b+7d=66$. Solving gives $d=6$ and $b=6$.

Alignment / evidence

A club sold 15 candles. Small candles cost \$6 and large candles cost \$10. The club collected \$118. Write and solve a system to determine how many small and large candles were sold.

Answer / solution

Let $s$ be small and $l$ large. $s+l=15$ and $6s+10l=118$. Solving gives $l=7$ and $s=8$.

Alignment / evidence

A parking area contains 18 cars and motorcycles altogether. There are 58 wheels total. Write and solve a system to determine the number of cars and motorcycles.

Answer / solution

Let $c$ be cars and $m$ motorcycles. $c+m=18$ and $4c+2m=58$. Solving gives $c=11$ and $m=7$.

Exit Ticket C 3

U4-S5-ETC-03 · Exit Ticket C · Mastery/Portfolio · 4.5-MG04-FIT-DIAGNOSE · Table + Model reasoning · constructed/open response

A proposed model is $y=3x+5$. Use the data to judge whether the model fits the whole set well enough, and identify the point that most weakens the fit.

xobserved y
18
211
314
417
527
Answer / solution

The model matches the first four points exactly, but at $x=5$ it predicts 20 while the observed value is 27. The point $(5,27)$ most weakens the linear fit, so the model is not a strong fit for the whole set without further investigation.

Alignment / evidence

Exit Ticket C 4

U4-S5-ETC-04 · Exit Ticket C · Mastery/Portfolio · 4.5-MG04-EXTRAPOLATION · Context + Model reasoning · constructed/open response

A linear model $P=12t+80$ fits a population data set for $0\le t\le 6$. A student uses it to predict the population at $t=40$. Explain why that prediction may be unreliable and state one kind of evidence that would support keeping or revising the model.

Answer / solution

The prediction is far outside the observed range, so it assumes the same linear trend continues much longer than the data support. Later-time data could be collected and compared with the model; systematic departures would be evidence to revise it.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Build and use linear models for real-world data, make predictions, and judge model fit for the question being asked.

Expected evidence/job: Recognizes extrapolation beyond the observed domain as a model limitation and identifies relevant new evidence for validating or revising the model.

Representation: Context + Model reasoning

Source basis: Original Unit Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework

04

V1 Question 5

U4-SUM-V1-Q05 · Secure Summative V1 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-SLOPE-CONTEXT · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A water level changes at a constant rate of 3 cm per minute. Explain what the slope means, predict the change after 12 minutes, and state one feature a graph of the situation would have.

Answer / solution

The slope is 3 cm/min. After 12 minutes the change is 36 cm. A graph would be a line with slope 3.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Interpret slope as constant rate of change across tables, graphs, and contexts and judge whether a rate is reasonable.

Expected evidence/job: Secure evidence for U4-MG01: Interpret slope as constant rate of change across tables, graphs, and contexts and judge whether a rate is reasonable.

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 4 Assessment Plan — Summative Evidence Plan + current Algebra Framework

V1 Question 6

U4-SUM-V1-Q06 · Secure Summative V1 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-GRAPH-EQ · Graph production · constructed/open response

Graph $y=0.5x+2$. Mark the y-intercept and one additional point determined from the slope, then interpret both features.

Answer / solution

Plot $(0,2)$ and use slope 0.5 to locate another point. The intercept is the starting value; the slope is the constant rate of change.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Graph linear functions and write linear equations from slope, intercepts, tables, graphs, and situations.

Expected evidence/job: Secure evidence for U4-MG02: Graph linear functions and write linear equations from slope, intercepts, tables, graphs, and situations.

Representation: Graph production

Source basis: Original Unit 4 Assessment Plan — Summative Evidence Plan + current Algebra Framework

V1 Question 7

U4-SUM-V1-Q07 · Secure Summative V1 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-COMPARE-GRAPH · Graph · constructed/open response

Two linear relationships are shown on the graph. Compare their rates of change and starting values. State which has the greater output for large positive x and justify using slope/intercept evidence.

Answer / solution

Relationship A has slope 1.5 and intercept 1; B has slope 0.5 and intercept 5. B starts greater, but A changes faster and eventually has the greater output.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare linear relationships shown in different representations using slope, intercepts, and contextual meaning.

Expected evidence/job: Secure evidence for U4-MG03: Compare linear relationships shown in different representations using slope, intercepts, and contextual meaning.

Representation: Graph

Source basis: Original Unit 4 Assessment Plan — Summative Evidence Plan + current Algebra Framework

V1 Question 8

U4-SUM-V1-Q08 · Secure Summative V1 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-FIT · Scatter plot · constructed/open response

A scatter plot with a fitted line is shown. Describe whether a linear model is reasonable, use the line to make one in-range prediction, and explain why an extreme far-outside-range prediction would be less trustworthy.

Answer / solution

The points follow an approximately increasing linear pattern, so a line is reasonable. A prediction within the displayed x-range can be read/estimated from the fitted line. Extrapolating far beyond the data is less trustworthy because the observed pattern may not continue.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Build and use linear models for real-world data, make predictions, and judge model fit for the question being asked.

Expected evidence/job: Secure evidence for U4-MG04: Build and use linear models for real-world data, make predictions, and judge model fit for the question being asked.

Representation: Scatter plot

Source basis: Original Unit 4 Assessment Plan — Summative Evidence Plan + current Algebra Framework

xRelationship B
01
25
49
Answer / solution

A has slope -0.5 and intercept 6; B has slope 2 and intercept 1. B changes faster in the positive direction; A has the greater starting value.

V2 Question 6

U4-SUM-V2-Q06 · Secure Summative V2 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-MODEL · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A linear model for a school fundraiser is $M=1.8d+11$, where $d$ is days and $M$ is hundreds of dollars. Predict $M$ at $d=5$ and interpret the slope and intercept.

Answer / solution

$M=1.8(5)+11=20$, so the model predicts \$2000. The slope means \$180 per day and the intercept means \$1100 at day 0.

Alignment / evidence

V2 Question 7

U4-SUM-V2-Q07 · Secure Summative V2 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-SLOPE-TABLE · Table + Context · constructed/open response

Use the table to determine the constant rate of change. Interpret its sign and size in the situation, and explain whether the rate is reasonable for the data shown.

time (h)distance from checkpoint (mi)
0-2
22
46
610
Answer / solution

Slope $=(2+2)/(2-0)=2$ miles per hour. The sign shows direction of change and the size matches every equal interval in the table.

U4-SUM-V5-Q01 · Secure Summative V5 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT5-RATE-REVERSE · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A hiking trail sign says a group gains 900 ft of elevation over 3 miles. Write the slope as a rate, interpret it, and construct another point that would lie on a graph with the same constant rate starting at 1200 ft.

Answer / solution

300 ft per mile; from $(0,1200)$ another point is $(3,2100)$, or any point consistent with that rate.

Alignment / evidence

V5 Question 2

U4-SUM-V5-Q02 · Secure Summative V5 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT5-LINE-FROM-TWO-POINTS · Equation · constructed/open response

A line passes through $(-2,7)$ and $(4,-5)$. Determine its slope, write an equation, and state the y-intercept.

Answer / solution

Slope $=(-5-7)/(4+2)=-2$. Using a point gives $y=-2x+3$; y-intercept 3.

Alignment / evidence

V5 Question 3

U4-SUM-V5-Q03 · Secure Summative V5 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT5-CROSSOVER-COMPARE · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

Plan A costs $18+4h$ dollars for h hours. Plan B costs $30+2h$. Compare starting costs and hourly rates, then determine when the plans cost the same and which plan is cheaper before and after that time.

Answer / solution

Plan A starts at \$18 and rises by \$4 per hour; Plan B starts at \$30 and rises by \$2 per hour. Solving $18+4h=30+2h$ gives $h=6$. Plan A is cheaper before 6 hours; Plan B is cheaper after 6 hours.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare linear relationships shown in different representations using slope, intercepts, and contextual meaning.

Expected evidence/job: Secure evidence for U4-MG03: Compare linear relationships shown in different representations using slope, intercepts, and contextual meaning.

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 4 Assessment Plan — Summative Evidence Plan + current Algebra Framework

V5 Question 4

U4-SUM-V5-Q04 · Secure Summative V5 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT5-MODEL-FROM-DATA · Table + Equation · constructed/open response

A club records members after each week: $(0,24),(2,32),(4,40),(6,48)$. Build a linear model, predict week 9, and state why the model is defensible for an in-range or nearby prediction.

Answer / solution

$M=4w+24$. At week 9, $M=60$. Equal intervals show a constant rate of 4 members/week, supporting a linear model for nearby predictions.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Build and use linear models for real-world data, make predictions, and judge model fit for the question being asked.

V5 Question 5

U4-SUM-V5-Q05 · Secure Summative V5 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT5-SLOPE-SIGN-CHOICE · Context · constructed/open response

A storage tank loses 18 gallons in 6 minutes. Determine the slope for volume versus time, explain why its sign matters, and describe a slope value that would contradict the observed situation.

Answer / solution

Slope $=-3$ gal/min. Negative shows volume decreases. A positive slope would contradict the stated loss.

Alignment / evidence

Construct a table with three points for $y=\frac32x-2$, then describe how those points would be placed on a graph and identify the intercept.

Answer / solution

For example x=0,2,4 gives y=-2,1,4. Plot those points; y-intercept is $(0,-2)$.

V5 Question 7

U4-SUM-V5-Q07 · Secure Summative V5 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT5-COMPARE-SCALE · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

Relationship A increases 12 units when x increases 3. Relationship B has equation $y=3x+10$. Which changes faster? Explain why comparing only one current output could be misleading.

Answer / solution

A slope is $12/3=4$, B slope is 3, so A changes faster. One output also depends on starting value, so it does not by itself determine rate.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare linear relationships shown in different representations using slope, intercepts, and contextual meaning.

Expected evidence/job: Secure evidence for U4-MG03: Compare linear relationships shown in different representations using slope, intercepts, and contextual meaning.

Representation: Context + Equation

V5 Question 8

U4-SUM-V5-Q08 · Secure Summative V5 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT5-MODEL-LIMITS · Model reasoning · constructed/open response

A linear model predicts the number of cafeteria lunches sold from enrollment. Explain one reason a linear model could fit the observed school-year data but fail if used to predict a school ten times larger.

Answer / solution

The relationship may be approximately linear only over the observed enrollment range; capacity, schedules, or participation rates can change, so extreme extrapolation may not preserve the same rate.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Build and use linear models for real-world data, make predictions, and judge model fit for the question being asked.

Secure Summative V6

V6 Question 1

U4-SUM-V6-Q01 · Secure Summative V6 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT6-GRAPH-SLOPE-TRIANGLE · Graph reasoning + Equation · constructed/open response

On a coordinate graph, a line passes through $(1,6)$ and $(5,-2)$. Compute the slope using a rise/run triangle, interpret the negative rate, and state whether reversing the point order changes the slope.

Answer / solution

Slope $=(-2-6)/(5-1)=-2$. The output falls 2 units per 1 input unit. Reversing both differences gives the same slope.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Interpret slope as constant rate of change across tables, graphs, and contexts and judge whether a rate is reasonable.

Expected evidence/job: Secure evidence for U4-MG01: Interpret slope as constant rate of change across tables, graphs, and contexts and judge whether a rate is reasonable.

Representation: Graph reasoning + Equation

V6 Question 2

U4-SUM-V6-Q02 · Secure Summative V6 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT6-CONTEXT-TO-EQ · Context + Equation + Table · constructed/open response

A bike rental starts at \$9 and costs \$6 per hour. Write the equation for total cost C after h hours, make a three-row table, and explain how the graph would show both parameters.

Answer / solution

$C=6h+9$. Example table: $(0,9),(1,15),(2,21)$. The graph crosses the C-axis at 9 and rises 6 per hour.

V6 Question 3

U4-SUM-V6-Q03 · Secure Summative V6 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT6-COMPARE-INTERSECT · Equation + Points · constructed/open response

Line A has equation $y=-x+8$. Line B passes through $(0,2)$ and $(3,8)$. Compare slopes and intercepts and determine the point where the lines have the same output.

Answer / solution

B slope 2 and intercept 2, so B is $y=2x+2$. Solving $-x+8=2x+2$ gives $x=2,y=6$. A starts higher; B increases faster.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare linear relationships shown in different representations using slope, intercepts, and contextual meaning.

Expected evidence/job: Secure evidence for U4-MG03: Compare linear relationships shown in different representations using slope, intercepts, and contextual meaning.

Representation: Equation + Points

V6 Question 4

U4-SUM-V6-Q04 · Secure Summative V6 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT6-SCATTER-DIAGNOSE · Scatter plot · constructed/open response

The scatter plot below is irregular even though a line of fit is drawn. Decide whether the line is a strong model and identify what in the data weakens a linear prediction.

Answer / solution

The points do not stay consistently close to a linear trend; large alternating deviations weaken the model, so precise linear predictions are not well supported.

V6 Question 5

U4-SUM-V6-Q05 · Secure Summative V6 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT6-RATE-UNIT-REASON · Context + Units · constructed/open response

A student reports the slope of distance versus time as 45 hours per mile. The data rise 135 miles over 3 hours. Correct the slope, including units, and explain the unit error.

Answer / solution

Slope is $135/3=45$ miles per hour. Distance change belongs in the numerator and time change in the denominator for distance versus time.

Alignment / evidence

V6 Question 6

U4-SUM-V6-Q06 · Secure Summative V6 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT6-GRAPH-FROM-FEATURES · Equation + Graph production · constructed/open response

Construct a linear equation and describe its graph if the y-intercept is -4 and the line rises 5 units for every run of 2 units.

Answer / solution

Slope $=5/2$, so one equation is $y=\frac52x-4$. Plot $(0,-4)$ and move right 2, up 5 for another point.

Relationship A is represented by the table $(0,7),(2,11),(4,15)$. Relationship B is described as starting at 3 and increasing 3 units per input. Compare both relationships using slope and intercept and state which is greater at x=10.

Answer / solution

A slope 2, intercept 7; B slope 3, intercept 3. At x=10, A=27 and B=33, so B is greater.

V6 Question 8

U4-SUM-V6-Q08 · Secure Summative V6 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-ALT6-MODEL-REVISION · Model reasoning · constructed/open response

A line models data well for x from 0 to 20, but a new point at x=21 lies far from the line. Explain two defensible next steps before making future predictions.

Answer / solution

Check whether the new point is an error/outlier and collect more nearby data; then reassess or revise the model if the relationship has changed.
